    content: # Optoelectronic Hybrid Neural Network for Image Style Transfer: A Comprehensive Technical Report

## 1. Overview: What is an Optoelectronic Hybrid Neural Network for Image Style Transfer?

An **optoelectronic hybrid neural network** for image style transfer is an emerging computational architecture that fuses **optical analog computing** (front-end) with **electronic digital neural networks** (back-end) to perform artistic style transfer — the process of rendering the content of one image in the artistic style of another. The core idea is to leverage the speed, parallelism, and energy efficiency of optical computing to accelerate the computationally intensive convolutional operations required for style extraction and synthesis, while relying on electronic neural networks for precise control, nonlinear activation, and iterative optimization.

**Key architectural distinction:** Unlike purely digital style transfer (e.g., Gatys et al., 2015) that runs entirely on GPUs, the hybrid approach offloads linear optical transformations (convolution, Fourier transforms, diffraction) to a metasurface-based optical front-end, dramatically reducing latency and power consumption. The electronic back-end handles tasks that are difficult or impossible to perform optically — nonlinear activations, batch normalization, training/inference optimization, and fine-grained pixel-level adjustments.

---

## 2. Component Breakdown

### 2.1 Metasurface

**Definition:** A metasurface is an ultrathin planar optical device composed of subwavelength-spaced nanostructures (meta-atoms) that collectively manipulate the phase, amplitude, and polarization of incident light.

**Role in the hybrid network:**
- Acts as the **physical neural network layer** — the meta-atoms encode trainable phase weights that modulate the optical wavefront.
- Implements **diffractive deep neural network (D2NN)** layers: Each metasurface layer acts as a collection of optical "neurons" that diffract and modulate light through free-space propagation to subsequent layers.
- Provides **ultra-compact form factor**: A single metasurface (thickness ~λ) replaces bulky diffractive optical elements, enabling chip-scale integration.

**Material considerations:**
- **TiO₂ (titanium dioxide):** High refractive index in visible, low loss — demonstrated in OENNs with 98.05% accuracy on MNIST.
- **Si (silicon):** Used in near-infrared, CMOS-compatible.
- **Dielectric resonators:** Low-loss, high-efficiency phase control across visible spectrum.

**Key advantage over conventional optics:** Metasurfaces allow precise subwavelength phase engineering (0 to 2π coverage) via geometric (Pancharatnam-Berry) phase or propagation phase mechanisms, enabling complex-valued weight matrices for optical neural networks.

### 2.2 Front Neural Network (Optical Front-End)

**Definition:** The optical front-end in an optoelectronic hybrid network is a physical optical computing layer (typically diffractive or metasurface-based) that performs high-speed, low-power linear transformations on input images before electronic processing.

**Architecture and operation:**
1. **Input plane:** An input image (e.g., spatial light modulator or amplitude mask) modulates a coherent light beam.
2. **Metasurface/diffractive layers:** Light passes through cascaded metasurface phase masks. Each metasurface imparts a spatially-varying phase shift. The optical field propagates between layers via diffraction.
3. **Output plane:** A CMOS camera or photodetector array captures the resulting intensity distribution.

**Mathematical model (angular spectrum formulation):**
The forward propagation through the k-th layer can be expressed as:
- Field modulation: `U_k'(x,y) = U_k(x,y) · t_k(x,y)` where `t_k` is the complex transmission of the metasurface
- Free-space propagation: `U_{k+1}(x,y) = ASP(U_k'(x,y), d_k)` where ASP is the angular spectrum propagation operator

**Functions performed optically:**
- **Convolution:** Metasurfaces can implement spatial convolution kernels directly, analogous to CNN convolutional layers.
- **Feature extraction:** Multiple wavelength/polarization channels can extract different features in parallel.
- **All-optical linear transformations:** Matrix-vector multiplications at the speed of light.

**Why optical front-end?**
- **Throughput:** Optical computing operates at the speed of light (~10^14 operations/sec)
- **Energy efficiency:** ~10^-15 J per operation vs. ~10^-12 J for electronic
- **Parallelism:** Inherent 2D parallel processing across millions of pixels simultaneously

### 2.3 Angular Spectrum Propagation (ASP)

**Definition:** The angular spectrum method is a rigorous scalar diffraction theory used to model the free-space propagation of light between diffractive layers. It is the backbone computational model for training and simulating diffractive neural networks.

**Mathematical formulation:**
Given an input field `U(x,y,0)` at plane z=0:
1. **Angular spectrum computation:**
   `A(f_x, f_y; 0) = ℱ{U(x,y,0)}`
   where ℱ denotes the 2D Fourier transform.

2. **Propagation transfer function:**
   `H(f_x, f_y; z) = exp(j·2π·z·√(1/λ² - f_x² - f_y²))`
   for propagating waves (f_x² + f_y² < 1/λ²), or exponential decay for evanescent waves.

3. **Field at distance z:**
   `U(x,y,z) = ℱ⁻¹{A(f_x, f_y; 0) · H(f_x, f_y; z)}`

**Why ASP for optical neural networks?**
- **Differentiability:** ASP is fully differentiable, making it compatible with gradient-based backpropagation (training via stochastic gradient descent).
- **Accuracy:** Unlike Fresnel diffraction, ASP has no paraxial approximation — valid for both near-field and far-field.
- **Physical fidelity:** Accurately models the wave propagation between metasurface layers, crucial for designing cascaded D2NNs.

**In style transfer context:** ASP enables the network to learn how optical fields carrying content and style features evolve through the metasurface stack, and the intensity at the output plane becomes the optically-computed feature map.

### 2.4 Computer-Generated Holography (CGH)

**Definition:** CGH is the computational technique for generating holograms — phase patterns that, when displayed on a spatial light modulator (SLM) or encoded on a metasurface, reconstruct a desired optical wavefront or image upon illumination.

**Relationship to style transfer:**
- **CGH as the rendering engine:** In an optoelectronic style transfer system, the CGH encodes the optically-processed feature maps into phase holograms that can reconstruct the stylized image.
- **Phase-only optimization:** Modern CGH uses iterative algorithms (Gerchberg-Saxton, Adam-based optimization) or neural networks to find the optimal phase pattern that minimizes the difference between the target image and the reconstructed image.
- **Camera-in-the-loop (CITL) training:** Recent advances (e.g., Neural Holography, SIGGRAPH 2020) use a physical camera to measure actual reconstructions, correcting for system aberrations.

**Deep learning for CGH:**
- **CNN-based hologram generation:** Networks with as few as 938 parameters can generate 4K CGH in ~18ms (real-time).
- **Propagation-adaptive solvers:** Spatial and Fourier neural operators that accept both target image AND propagation distance as inputs, enabling dynamic refocusing.
- **Complex amplitude encoding:** Neural networks can directly predict phase patterns that achieve high-fidelity image reconstructions with full color capability.

**Role in style transfer pipeline:**
1. The electronic back-end computes the target stylized image features.
2. CGH algorithms (neural or iterative) encode these features into a phase hologram.
3. The metasurface/SLM reconstructs the final stylized image optically.

### 2.5 Style Transfer (Core Algorithm)

**Definition (Gatys et al., 2015):** Neural style transfer is an optimization-based technique that uses a pre-trained convolutional neural network (typically VGG-19) to separate and recombine content and style from two different images.

**Core components:**

1. **Content representation:**
   - Extracted from higher layers of a CNN (e.g., VGG-19 conv4_2, conv5_2)
   - Content loss: `L_content = ½ ||F_l(X_gen) - F_l(X_content)||²` where `F_l` are feature maps at layer l

2. **Style representation:**
   - Captured by Gram matrices of feature maps across multiple CNN layers
   - Gram matrix: `G_l[i,j] = Σ_k F_l(i,k) · F_l(j,k)` where k indexes spatial positions
   - Style loss: `L_style = Σ_l w_l · (1/4N_l²M_l²) · ||G_l(X_gen) - G_l(X_style)||²`

3. **Total loss minimization:**
   `L_total = α · L_content + β · L_style`
   Optimized via gradient descent on the input image pixels, not on the network weights.

**Evolution:**
- **Iterative optimization (Gatys):** Slow but high-quality; requires ~500 iterations.
- **Feed-forward networks (Johnson et al., 2016):** Real-time style transfer using a trained generator network (Perceptual Losses).
- **Arbitrary style transfer:** Adaptive Instance Normalization (AdaIN), StyleGAN-based approaches — enable any style to be applied without per-style training.

**Role in the optoelectronic hybrid:**
- The style and content loss framework can be partially computed in the optical domain.
- Metasurface front-ends can optically compute the feature correlations (Gram matrices) at high speed.
- The electronic back-end performs the iterative optimization or runs the feed-forward generator.

---

## 3. How Metasurface Phase Arrays Can Be Optimized for Multi-Wavelength Image Style Transfer

Multi-wavelength style transfer is critical for **color image processing**, where content and style images are in color (RGB). Processing each wavelength channel separately or simultaneously requires careful engineering of metasurface dispersion.

### 3.1 Key Challenges

1. **Chromatic dispersion:** Metasurface phase response varies with wavelength — a phase designed for λ=633nm will produce a different wavefront at λ=532nm or λ=450nm.
2. **Wavelength-multiplexed task competition:** Different wavelengths may need to extract different features or process different color channels, creating cross-talk.
3. **Phase wrapping:** The 0-2π phase coverage condition differs across wavelengths.

### 3.2 Optimization Strategies

#### A. Inverse Design with Adjoint Optimization

**Approach:**
- Treat metasurface design as a **gradient-based inverse problem**.
- Use the **adjoint method** (analogous to backpropagation in neural networks) to compute gradients of a loss function (e.g., style transfer quality metric) with respect to meta-atom geometry.
- Simultaneously simulate the optical response at all target wavelengths using RCWA (Rigorous Coupled-Wave Analysis) or FDTD (Finite-Difference Time-Domain).

**Key papers:** 
- "Computational inverse design for cascaded systems of metasurface optics" (arXiv:1906.10753) — demonstrates co-design of metasurface phase arrays with downstream processing.
- "Large-scale parametrized metasurface design using adjoint optimization" (Mansouree et al., 2021, ACS Photonics) — scales to millions of meta-atoms.

**Advantage for multi-wavelength:** Optimization can be formulated as a multi-objective problem minimizing the weighted sum of losses at all wavelengths simultaneously.

#### B. Dispersive Phase Compensation

**Physical principle:** Meta-atoms with high-Q resonances provide strong dispersion. By engineering multiple resonant modes (electric dipole, magnetic dipole, higher-order modes) within each meta-atom, one can achieve **wavelength-dependent phase shifts** that compensate for natural chromatic dispersion.

**Technique (Arbabi et al., 2015; Wang et al., 2018):**
- **Multi-resonance meta-atoms:** Design nanostructures that support multiple overlapping Mie resonances.
- **Dispersive phase compensation:** The total phase shift `φ(λ, x, y) = φ_geometric(x,y) + φ_resonant(λ, x, y)` where the resonant part cancels the propagation-induced dispersion.
- **Achromatic operation:** Demonstrated for metalenses — deflection and focusing at three visible wavelengths with the same angle/focal length.

**Application to style transfer:** A metasurface with compensated dispersion can process all RGB channels identically, preserving the intended style transformation across color components.

#### C. Wavelength Multiplexing via Meta-Atom Design

**Approach (phase correlation method; Xiang et al., 2024, Optics Letters):**
- Use a **multi-layer perceptron (MLP)** to learn the correlation between phase responses at different wavelengths.
- Train the MLP to map a target phase pattern at one wavelength to the corresponding phase patterns at other wavelengths.
- This reduces multi-channel design to a single-channel optimization problem.

**Demonstrated:**
- Dual-wavelength multiplexed diffractive neural network (DW-MDNN) for simultaneous classification at two wavelengths.
- Multi-wavelength color holography (see Section 2.4) — independent RGB holograms from a single metasurface.

**For style transfer:** Each wavelength channel (R, G, B) can be assigned independent phase patterns that process different aspects of style or content, then recombined at the output.

#### D. Polarization-Wavelength Joint Multiplexing

**Technique (Luo et al., 2022, Light: Science & Applications):**
- Metasurfaces with anisotropic meta-atoms (e.g., elliptical nanopillars) independently control phase for orthogonal linear polarizations (x and y).
- Combined with wavelength selectivity, this yields **4 independent channels** (2 polarizations × 2 wavelengths).
- Demonstrated on-chip multiplexed diffractive neural networks (MDNN) integrated on CMOS sensors with 6.25 × 10⁶ neurons/mm².

**For style transfer:** 
- One polarization channel can process content features, another processes style features.
- Different wavelengths handle different color channels, enabling true color style transfer entirely in the optical domain.

#### E. Data-Driven Neural Network Inverse Design

**Method:**
1. **Forward model:** Train a deep neural network as a surrogate for Maxwell solvers (FDTD/RCWA) to predict the far-field response of a metasurface given its geometry.
2. **Inverse design network:** Train a second network (often a generative model, VAE, or GAN) to predict meta-atom geometries that produce a desired phase profile at multiple wavelengths.
3. **End-to-end co-optimization:** Jointly optimize the metasurface geometry AND the electronic processing network weights using a differentiable pipeline.

**Key works:**
- "Neural network enabled metasurface design for phase manipulation" (Optics Express, 2021) — DNN handles six geometric parameters for phase prediction.
- "End-to-end metasurface inverse design for single-shot multi-channel imaging" — co-optimizes nanophotonic front-end with image processing back-end.

#### F. Multi-Wavelength Diffractive D2NN Weighting Method

**Approach (Feng et al., HOLOEYE):**
- Train a **single diffractive neural network** that accepts multi-wavelength input.
- Use **weight coefficients** that depend on wavelength: `W_total(λ) = Σ w_i(λ) · W_i` where `W_i` are base phase masks and `w_i(λ)` are wavelength-dependent scaling factors.
- During training, randomly sample different wavelengths so the network learns a wavelength-agnostic or wavelength-aware transformation.

**Performance:** 3-layer MW-D2NN achieves 83.3% classification accuracy with multi-wavelength illumination, laying groundwork for broadband optical inference.

### 3.3 Complete Multi-Wavelength Optimization Pipeline for Style Transfer

**Step-by-step integrated approach:**

```
Phase 1: Task Definition
├── Define style transfer loss: L_total = α·L_content(RGB) + β·L_style(RGB)  
├── Choose operating wavelengths: λ_R, λ_G, λ_B (e.g., 450, 532, 633 nm)
└── Specify metasurface constraints: fabrication limits (minimum feature size, aspect ratio)

Phase 2: Design Optimization
├── Initialize meta-atom library (discrete or continuous geometry parameters)
├── For each wavelength:
│   └── Compute target phase map using differentiable optical propagation model (ASP)
├── Multi-objective optimization:
│   ├── Adjoint gradients: ∂L/∂φ(λ_k) for all k wavelengths simultaneously
│   ├── Physics-constrained via RCWA/FDTD forward model
│   └── Regularization: enforce smooth phase transitions, fabrication constraints
└── Output: Cascaded metasurface phase masks (single or multilayer)

Phase 3: Electronic Back-End Co-Design
├── Train electronic network (e.g., VGG-19 or lightweight CNN) for style/content loss
├── Learn to compensate for optical front-end imperfections
├── Camera-in-the-loop fine-tuning: measure physical output, adjust both
│   metasurface geometry and electronic weights
└── Deploy: metasurface fabricated; electronic processing on FPGA/ASIC

Phase 4: Inference
├── Input content & style images → SLM/modulator
├── Optical front-end (metasurface): all-optical feature extraction at light speed
├── Detector array captures optically processed features
└── Electronic back-end: computes final stylized output with minimal processing
```

---

## 4. State-of-the-Art References and Key Metrics

| Component | Key Work | Metric / Result |
|---|---|---|
| **All-optical D2NN** | Lin et al., Science 361, 1004 (2018) | First experimental all-optical diffractive neural network; THz regime |
| **Metasurface OENN** | Qiu et al., LPR 16, 2200732 (2022) | Single TiO₂ metasurface + FC layer: 98.05% on MNIST |
| **On-chip MDNN** | Luo et al., Light Sci. Appl. 11, 158 (2022) | 6.25×10⁶ neurons/mm²; polarization multiplexed |
| **Multi-wavelength D2NN** | Duan et al., Nanophotonics 12, 893 (2023) | 2-task and 4-task parallel classification; 4 spectral channels |
| **CGH with CITL** | Peng et al., SIGGRAPH 2020 (Neural Holography) | 1080p full-color holographic video, real-time |
| **Neural CGH** | Chang et al., Sci. Rep. 13 (2023) | 4K CGH in 18ms, 938-parameter CNN |
| **Phase correlation multiplexing** | Xiang et al., Opt. Lett. 50, 1989 (2025) | MLP-based dual-wavelength MDNN + color holography |
| **DANTE training** | Nature Commun. 14 (2023) | 150M neuron ONN trained on ImageNet |
| **Neural Style Transfer** | Gatys et al., CVPR 2016 | Foundational algorithm; VGG-19 based |

---

## 5. Advantages and Future Directions

### Advantages of the Optoelectronic Hybrid Approach

| Aspect | Electronic-Only | Hybrid (Optoelectronic) |
|---|---|---|
| **Energy consumption** | ~10⁻¹² J/op (GPU) | ~10⁻¹⁵ J/op (optical) |
| **Speed** | ~10¹² ops/sec (GPU) | ~10¹⁴ ops/sec (optical) |
| **Parallelism** | Limited by memory bandwidth | Inherent 2D parallel processing |
| **Footprint** | Large (GPU cluster) | Chip-scale integration possible |
| **Style transfer quality** | State-of-the-art | Emerging; comparable on classification tasks |

### Future Research Directions

1. **Nonlinear optical activation functions:** Current hybrid systems rely on electronic back-ends for nonlinearities. Developing all-optical nonlinearities (Kerr effect, saturable absorption) would enable fully optical style transfer.
2. **Programmable/tunable metasurfaces:** Using phase-change materials (e.g., GST, Sb₂Se₃) or liquid crystals to enable real-time reconfiguration of the metasurface for dynamic style parameters.
3. **End-to-end differentiable training:** Integrating full-wave electromagnetic simulation (FDTD/RCWA) into the training loop for truly end-to-end co-optimization of metasurface and electronic weights.
4. **All-analog photoelectronic chips:** Novel architectures (e.g., Chen et al., Nature 2023) that combine analog optical and electronic processing on a single chip for ultra-high-speed vision tasks.
5. **3D style transfer and holographic displays:** Combining CGH with multi-wavelength metasurface optimization for full-parallax, full-color 3D stylized scenes.
6. **Learned metasurface initialization:** Using generative AI to propose initial metasurface designs that are then refined through physics-based optimization.

---

## 6. Conclusion

An optoelectronic hybrid neural network for image style transfer represents the convergence of **nanophotonics (metasurfaces)**, **physical optics (angular spectrum propagation)**, **computer-generated holography**, and **deep learning (style transfer algorithms)**. The key insight is that linear optical operations — convolutions, Fourier transforms, feature correlations — can be performed at the speed of light in an ultrathin metasurface layer, while nonlinear processing, iterative refinement, and final image generation are handled by an electronic co-processor.

For multi-wavelength (color) style transfer, metasurface phase arrays can be optimized through:
1. **Adjoint-based inverse design** with multi-objective loss formulations
2. **Dispersive phase compensation** using multi-resonance meta-atoms
3. **Wavelength multiplexing** via phase correlation neural networks
4. **Polarization-wavelength joint multiplexing** for maximum channel capacity
5. **End-to-end differentiable pipelines** that co-optimize optics and electronics

This emerging paradigm promises orders-of-magnitude improvements in speed and energy efficiency over purely digital style transfer, enabling real-time artistic video processing, interactive creative tools, and ultra-low-power edge-device deployment.

## Summary: Optoelectronic Hybrid Neural Network for Image Style Transfer

### What It Is
An **optoelectronic hybrid neural network** for style transfer fuses an **optical front-end** (metasurface-based diffractive layers) with an **electronic digital back-end** (CNN) to perform artistic style transfer — applying the style of one image to the content of another — at dramatically higher speed and lower energy than purely electronic systems.

### Component Breakdown

| Component | Role |
|---|---|
| **Metasurface** | Ultra-thin nanophotonic surface that acts as the optical "neuron" layer, modulating light phase/amplitude at subwavelength scale to perform linear transformations (convolutions, diffractions) at the speed of light |
| **Front Neural Network (Optical)** | Cascaded metasurface layers + free-space propagation that optically compute feature extraction, convolution, and correlation operations in parallel across millions of pixels |
| **Angular Spectrum Propagation (ASP)** | Rigorous diffraction model (Fourier-based) used to simulate light propagation between layers; **fully differentiable**, enabling gradient-based training of metasurface weights via backpropagation |
| **Computer-Generated Holography (CGH)** | Computational method for encoding optical wavefronts into holographic phase patterns; used to render the final stylized image; modern neural CGH achieves 4K resolution in ~18ms |
| **Style Transfer (Gatys et al.)** | Optimizes a target image to minimize content loss (CNN feature distance from content image) + style loss (Gram matrix correlation distance from style image) |

### Multi-Wavelength Optimization of Metasurface Phase Arrays

For **color style transfer** (RGB), metasurface optimization employs six complementary strategies:

1. **Adjoint inverse design** — Gradient-based optimization simultaneously minimizing losses at λ_R, λ_G, λ_B
2. **Dispersive phase compensation** — Multi-resonance meta-atoms that cancel chromatic aberration
3. **Phase correlation MLP** — Neural network learns correlations between phase maps at different wavelengths
4. **Polarization-wavelength joint multiplexing** — Up to 4 independent channels per meta-atom
5. **Data-driven inverse design** — Surrogate neural networks replace FDTD for rapid geometry optimization
6. **Multi-wavelength D2NN weighting** — Wavelength-dependent weight coefficients in a single diffractive network

### Key Advantage
Optical computing offers ~10⁻¹⁵ J/op vs. ~10⁻¹² J/op (electronic), with inherent 2D parallelism, enabling potentially **100-1000× improvement** in energy efficiency for style transfer tasks.

from dotenv import load_dotenv
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from FrontNetwork import FrontEnd
from pathlib import Path
from parameters import kwargs
from Coder import Coding

load_dotenv()
folder_path = kwargs.get("folder_path")

FWHM_QUERY = f"""Write a function that calculates the full width at half maximum (FWHM) of a waveform.
Input: A one-dimensional waveform array.
Output: The FWHM value, in nanometers.

Please ensure:

1. The spacing between adjacent points in the input waveform array is 160 nm. Therefore, the computed FWHM should be multiplied by 160 nm to obtain the result in nanometers.
2. Define the file name of the code as FWHM.py.
3. Define the function name as func_FWHM."""

SSIM_QUERY = f"""Write a function that measures the similarity between two images using the SSIM (Structural Similarity Index) metric.
Inputs:
1. An image with dimensions **[{kwargs["Meta_N"]},{kwargs["Meta_N"]},3]**.
2. Another image with the same dimensions **[{kwargs["Meta_N"]},{kwargs["Meta_N"]},3]**.
Output:
The SSIM value (a scalar).

Please ensure:

1. Define the file name of the code as SSIM.py.
2. Define the function name as func_SSIM.
3. Do not include any specific symbols (e.g., ✓, →, ★) or emoji/emoticons."""

PSNR_QUERY = f"""Write a function that measures the similarity between two images using the PSNR (peak signal-to-noise ratio) metric.
Inputs:
1. An image with dimensions **[{kwargs["Meta_N"]},{kwargs["Meta_N"]},3]**.
2. Another image with the same dimensions **[{kwargs["Meta_N"]},{kwargs["Meta_N"]},3]**.
Output:
The PSNR value (a scalar with unit in dB).

Please ensure:

1. Define the file name of the code as PSNR.py.
2. Define the function name as func_PSNR.
3. Do not include any specific symbols (e.g., ✓, →, ★) or emoji/emoticons."""

def read_file(file_name):
    file_name = Path(r"D:\work\MetaDesign\Metasurface") / file_name
    with open(file_name, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
        code_content = f.read()
    return code_content

GENERATOR_QUERY = """Write a function that calculates the content loss between input_img and content_img, and the style_loss between input_img and style_img.
Inputs:
- input_img: The style_transferred images with dimensions of [batch, 3, {Meta_N}, {Meta_N}].
- content_img: The content images with dimensions of [batch, 3, {Meta_N}, {Meta_N}].
- style_img: The style images with dimensions of [batch, 3, {Meta_N}, {Meta_N}].
Output:
- content_score: Content loss value with dimensions of [batch].
- style_score: Style loss value with dimensions of [batch].

The reference code:
```Python
{code}
```

Please ensure:
1. Preserve the content of the reference code as much as possible.
2. Only return the content loss value and the style loss value. Do not optimize any parameters.
3. Define the file name of the code as StyleLoss.py.
4. Define the main function as **func_loss**."""

class Evaluate(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, task, output):
        super(Evaluate, self).__init__()
        self.task = task
        self.frontNet = FrontEnd(task)
        self.output = output

    def Focus(self):
        input = self.frontNet()
        in_intensity = input[0][0].sum().detach()
        efficiency = []
        FWHM = []
        Coding('FWHM', FWHM_QUERY)
        from FWHM import func_FWHM
        for i in range(3):
            idx = torch.argmax(self.output[0,i])  # 展平后的索引
            x, y = torch.unravel_index(idx, self.output[0,i].shape)  # 转换为二维坐标
            efficiency.append((100*self.output[0,i,x-10:x+11,y-10:y+11].sum()/in_intensity).item())
            FWHM.append(func_FWHM(self.output[0,i,:,y].detach().numpy()).item())
        return f'At the red/green/blue lights, the efficiency of the designed metalens (defined as output intensity divided by input intensity) are {efficiency}%, and the FWHM are {FWHM} nm, respectively.'

    def Holography(self):
        output = self.output
        output = output[:, :, output.shape[2]//4: 3*output.shape[2]//4, output.shape[3]//4: 3*output.shape[3]//4] # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        target = torch.load(folder_path + "/Holography/trainData.pt")
        img_arr1 = target.permute(0, 2, 3, 1).detach().numpy()
        img_arr2 = output.permute(0, 2, 3, 1).detach().numpy()
        Coding('SSIM', SSIM_QUERY)
        Coding('PSNR', PSNR_QUERY)
        from SSIM import func_SSIM
        from PSNR import func_PSNR
        SSIM_arr=[]
        PSNR_arr=[]
        for i in range(len(img_arr1)):
            SSIM_arr.append(func_SSIM(img_arr1[i], img_arr2[i]).tolist())
            PSNR_arr.append(func_PSNR(img_arr1[i], img_arr2[i]).tolist())
        return f'The SSIM scores between the holography images and the target images at {len(img_arr1)} focal lengths are {SSIM_arr}.\nThe PSNR scores between the holography images and the target images at {len(img_arr1)} focal lengths are {PSNR_arr}.'

    def Generator(self):
        Coding('StyleLoss', GENERATOR_QUERY.format(Meta_N=kwargs["Meta_N"],code=read_file('styleCode.py')))
        from StyleLoss import func_loss
        batch, channel, x, y = self.output.shape
        content_img = torch.load(folder_path + "/Generator/contentImg.pt")
        style_img = torch.load(folder_path + "/Generator/styleImg.pt")
        output = torch.load(folder_path + "/Generator/target.pt")
        content_score, style_score = func_loss(output, content_img, style_img)
        return f'For the content image, {batch} style-transferred images are generated. Their content loss values with respect to the content image are {content_score}, and their style_loss with respect to the style image are {style_score}, respectively.'

    def forward(self):
        if self.task == "Focus":
            return self.Focus()
        elif self.task == "Holography":
            return self.Holography()
        elif self.task == "Generator":
            return self.Generator()
        else:
            return f"The task {self.task} is not implemented."

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from dotenv import load_dotenv
from parameters import kwargs
import numpy as np
from Coder import Coding

load_dotenv()
folder_path = kwargs.get("folder_path")

CODING_QUERY = f"""Write PyTorch code to implement image style transfer.
Define the main function as **run_style_transfer**, which accepts two input tensors:
- **content**: shape [batch, 3, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}]
- **style**: shape [batch, 3, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}]

For each content-style pair (treated as individual images with batch dimension 1), generate a style-transferred image of shape [1, 3, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}].
Concatenate all generated images along the batch dimension and return a tensor of shape [batch, 3, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}, {kwargs["Meta_N"]}].

Please ensure:
1. Preserve the style of the retrievable code as much as possible.
2. The style_weight is **1e6**, content_weight is **1**, and optimize for 300 epochs.
3. Save the code in a file named styleCode.py. The code file should not have any parent folder; that is, the file path should be directly styleCode.py, not x/styleCode.py.
4. Do not include any specific symbols (e.g., ✓, →, ★) or emoji/emoticons."""

class FocalLoss(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self,alpha=0.5,gamma=2,logits=False,reduce=True):
        super(FocalLoss,self).__init__()
        self.alpha=alpha
        self.gamma=gamma
        self.logits=logits
        self.reduce=reduce

    def forward(self,inputs,targets):
        if self.logits:
            BCE_loss=F.binary_cross_entropy_with_logits(inputs,targets,reduce=False)
        else:
            BCE_loss=F.binary_cross_entropy(inputs,targets,reduce=False)
        pt=torch.exp(-BCE_loss)
        F_loss=self.alpha*(1-pt)**self.gamma*BCE_loss

        if self.reduce:
            return torch.mean(F_loss)
        else:
            return F_loss

class OptimLoss(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, task):
        super(OptimLoss, self).__init__()
        self.task = task
        if self.task == "Generator":
            try:
                self.transfer_target = torch.load(folder_path + "/Generator/target.pt")
                print('=' * 20, " Load style-transferred images ", "=" * 20)
            except:
                Coding('styleCode', CODING_QUERY)
                from styleCode import run_style_transfer
                content_img = torch.load(folder_path + "/Generator/contentImg.pt")
                style_img = torch.load(folder_path + "/Generator/styleImg.pt")
                print('Transferring image style, this may take some time for large images...')
                self.transfer_target = run_style_transfer(content_img, style_img)
                torch.save(self.transfer_target, folder_path + "/Generator/target.pt")

    def Focus(self, output):
        output = output[:, :, output.shape[2] // 4: 3 * output.shape[2] // 4, output.shape[3] // 4: 3 * output.shape[3] // 4]  # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        target = torch.load(folder_path + "/Focus/target.pt")
        loss = FocalLoss()(output/30000, target)
        return loss

    def Holography(self, output):
        output = output[:, :, output.shape[2] // 4: 3 * output.shape[2] // 4, output.shape[3] // 4: 3 * output.shape[3] // 4]  # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        target = torch.load(folder_path + "/Holography/trainData.pt") # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        mask = torch.load(folder_path + "/Holography/masks.pt") # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        output = torch.mul(output, mask)
        loss = nn.MSELoss()(output.double(), target.double())
        return loss

    def Generator(self, output):
        output = output[:, :, output.shape[2] // 4: 3 * output.shape[2] // 4, output.shape[3] // 4: 3 * output.shape[3] // 4]  # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        loss = nn.MSELoss()(output.double(), self.transfer_target.double())
        return loss

    def forward(self,output):
        if self.task == "Focus":
            return self.Focus(output)
        elif self.task == "Holography":
            return self.Holography(output)
        elif self.task == "Generator":
            return self.Generator(output)
        else:
            return f"The task {self.task} is not implemented."

from dotenv import load_dotenv
import os
import torch
from parameters import kwargs
from DataFlow import MetaOptim
from LossFunction import OptimLoss
from Visualization import Visual
import math
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np
from deepagents import create_deep_agent
from langchain.tools import tool
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import InMemorySaver
from Performance import Evaluate
from FileManager import read_file,read_folder
from Coder import internet_search
from Researcher import search_paper

load_dotenv()
checkpointer = InMemorySaver()
folder_path = kwargs.get("folder_path")

OPTIMIZER_SYSTEM_PROMPT = """You are a metasurface optimizer. You need to optimize metasurface's phase array for specific tasks and evaluate the performance.

You have access to tools:
1. read_file: To read the content of a local file. This is the only way to access local files. 
The only files you can directly access without tools are those in your virtual file system, which are private and accessible only to your.
Files created by other agents or existing project code stored in local directories are not directly accessible. To access these files, you must use this tool. 
2. read_folder: To retrieve all file names in a specified folder. 
This is useful for verifying whether files have been successfully saved during training or testing processes.
3. train: To optimize metasurface's phase array. 
4. test: To evaluate the performance of the optimized metasurface.
5. search_paper: For academic paper search.
6. internet_search: For web search.

The optimization code comprises four modules:
1. FrontNetwork: To generate amplitude arrays for different incident frequencies on the metasurface surface. For the **Focus** and **Holography** tasks, the incident waves are plane waves. For the **Generator** task, the incident wave is an information-carrying amplitude that has been processed by a neural network.
2. AS (Angular Spectrum method): To calculate propagation dynamics of light from the metasurface to the focal plane.
3. DataFlow: To package the entire data processing pipeline to a PyTorch neural network for subsequent optimization.
4. LossFunction: Task-specific loss functions.

Please ensure:
1. A group of sub-Agents will assist you to write code. **You are NOT required to write any code by yourself.** You just need to analyze the code in the report.
The report file should not have any parent folder; that is, the file path should be directly y.md, not x/y.md.
2. Only analyze the codes and reports directly relevant to the current task. For example, if solving the 'Holography' task, **do not** analyze code for 'Focus' or 'Generator'. 
3. After completing each reasoning step, always conclude by calling **write_file** to output a report."""

TEST_RETURN = """Performance of metasurface is evaluated.

{performance}

The optimized phase array is saved as {phase}, and the evaluation results are saved in the folder {results_folder}."""

@tool
def train(
        task: str,
        frequencies: list[int]
):
    """To optimize metasurface's phase array.

    Input:
        task: The specific task to be implemented, 'Focus', 'Holography' or 'Generator'.
        - 'Focus': RGB Metalens design.
        - 'Holography': Computer-generated holography.
        - 'Generator': Image Style Transfer.

        frequencies: Incident frequency array in THz."""
    try:
        os.makedirs(folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel")
    except:
        pass

    best_loss = kwargs["best_loss"]
    network = MetaOptim(task, frequencies)
    f_loss = OptimLoss(task)
    optimizer = torch.optim.Rprop(network.parameters(), lr=2e-4)

    # Initialize network parameters
    try:
        network.load_state_dict(torch.load(folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel/network.pt"))
        optimizer.load_state_dict(torch.load(folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel/optimizer.pt"))
        print('=' * 20, " Load pretrained model ", "=" * 20)
    except:
        pass

    for epoch in range(kwargs["epochs"]):
        outputs = network()
        loss = f_loss(outputs)
        optimizer.zero_grad()
        loss.backward(torch.ones_like(loss))
        optimizer.step()
        print('Epoch [{}/{}] \tLoss: {}\t.'.format(epoch + 1, kwargs["epochs"], loss))
        if loss < best_loss:
            best_loss = loss
            torch.save(network.state_dict(), folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel/network.pt")
            torch.save(optimizer.state_dict(), folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel/optimizer.pt")

    return f"The trained model is saved in the file {os.path.join(folder_path, task, "SavedModel")}"

@tool
def test(
        task: str,
        frequencies: list[int]
):
    """To evaluate the performance of the optimized metasurface, and upload optimized phase array.

    Input:
        task: The specific task to be implemented, 'Focus', 'Holography' or 'Generator'.
        - 'Focus': RGB Metalens design.
        - 'Holography': Computer-generated holography.
        - 'Generator': Image Style Transfer.

        frequencies: Incident frequency array in THz.

    Returns:
        The optimized phase array."""
    network = MetaOptim(task, frequencies)
    network.load_state_dict(torch.load(folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel/network.pt"))
    print('=' * 20, " Load pretrained model ", "=" * 20)

    phase = network.phase.detach().numpy()%(2*math.pi)
    phase = phase[phase.shape[0]//4: 3*phase.shape[0]//4, phase.shape[1]//4: 3*phase.shape[1]//4]
    plt.imshow(phase, cmap='gray')
    plt.colorbar()
    plt.axis('off')
    plt.savefig(folder_path + f"/{task}/SavedModel/phase.png",dpi=500, bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
    plt.show()
    np.save('phase.npy', phase)

    outputs = network()
    Visual(task)(outputs)
    performance = Evaluate(task,outputs)()
    return TEST_RETURN.format(performance=performance,phase=folder_path+'\phase.npy', results_folder=os.path.join(folder_path, task, "SavedModel"))

optimizer_agent = create_deep_agent(
    model="deepseek:deepseek-chat",
    checkpointer=checkpointer,
    system_prompt=OPTIMIZER_SYSTEM_PROMPT,
    tools=search_paper+[internet_search, train, test, read_file, read_folder],
)

import torch
from parameters import kwargs
import torch.nn as nn
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np
from matplotlib.colors import LinearSegmentedColormap

folder_path = kwargs.get("folder_path")

class Visual(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, task):
        super(Visual, self).__init__()
        self.task = task

    def Focus(self, output):
        output = output[0, :, output.shape[2]//4: 3*output.shape[2]//4, output.shape[3]//4: 3*output.shape[3]//4] # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        color = ['#FE0000', '#3EFF00', '#00B5FF']
        for i in range(output.shape[0]):
            plt.figure(figsize=(4, 4))
            out = output[i]
            idx = torch.argmax(out)  # 展平后的索引
            x, y = torch.unravel_index(idx, out.shape)  # 转换为二维坐标
            plt.imshow(out[x-10:x+11,y-10:y+11].detach().numpy(), cmap=LinearSegmentedColormap.from_list('custom_cmap', ['#000000', color[i]], N=100))
            plt.colorbar(fraction=0.046, pad=0.04)
            plt.axis('off')
            plt.savefig(folder_path + "/Focus/SavedModel/2D_"+str(i)+".png",dpi=500, bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
            plt.show()

        plt.figure(figsize=(4, 3))
        plt.xlim(0,512)
        for i in range(output.shape[0]):
            intensity = output[i,:,y].detach().numpy()
            plt.plot(intensity/intensity.max(), color = color[i])
        plt.gca().spines['top'].set_visible(False)
        plt.gca().spines['right'].set_visible(False)
        plt.savefig(folder_path + "/Focus/SavedModel/1D_"+str(i)+".png",dpi=500, bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0, transparent=True)
        plt.show()

    def Holography(self,output):
        output = output[:, :, output.shape[2]//4: 3*output.shape[2]//4, output.shape[3]//4: 3*output.shape[3]//4] # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N
        out = output.permute(0, 2, 3, 1).squeeze(0).detach().numpy()

        for i in range(len(out)):
            plt.figure(figsize=(6, 6))
            plt.imshow(out[i])
            plt.axis("off")
            plt.tight_layout()
            plt.savefig(folder_path + f"/Holography/SavedModel/Part{i}.png",dpi=500, bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
            plt.show()

        plt.figure(figsize=(6, 6))
        mask = torch.load(folder_path + "/Holography/masks.pt")
        output = torch.mul(output,mask).permute(0, 2, 3, 1).sum(0).detach().numpy()
        plt.imshow(output)
        plt.axis("off")
        plt.savefig(folder_path + "/Holography/SavedModel/Holography.png",dpi=500, bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
        plt.show()

    def Generator(self,output):
        out = output[:, :, output.shape[2]//4: 3*output.shape[2]//4, output.shape[3]//4: 3*output.shape[3]//4].permute(0, 2, 3, 1).detach().numpy() # batch, 3, Meta_N, Meta_N

        for i in range(out.shape[0]):
            plt.figure(figsize=(6, 6))
            plt.imshow(out[i])
            plt.axis("off")
            plt.savefig(folder_path + "/Generator/SavedModel/transferPic"+str(i)+".png",dpi=500, bbox_inches='tight', pad_inches=0)
            plt.show()

    def forward(self,output):
        if self.task == "Focus":
            return self.Focus(output)
        elif self.task == "Holography":
            return self.Holography(output)
        elif self.task == "Generator":
            return self.Generator(output)
        else:
            return f"The task {self.task} is not implemented."

import torch.nn as nn


class EncoderNet(nn.Module):
    """
    Lightweight encoder network that downsamples input from [batch, 3, 512, 512] to [batch, 3, 32, 32].
    This represents a 16x downsampling (512/32 = 16).
    """
    
    def __init__(self):
        super(EncoderNet, self).__init__()
        
        # Encoder blocks with progressive downsampling
        # Each block reduces spatial dimensions by 2x
        
        # Block 1: 512x512 -> 256x256
        self.block1 = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(3, 32, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
            nn.Conv2d(32, 32, kernel_size=3, stride=1, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        )
        
        # Block 2: 256x256 -> 128x128
        self.block2 = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(32, 64, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
            nn.Conv2d(64, 64, kernel_size=3, stride=1, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        )
        
        # Block 3: 128x128 -> 64x64
        self.block3 = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(64, 128, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
            nn.Conv2d(128, 128, kernel_size=3, stride=1, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        )
        
        # Block 4: 64x64 -> 32x32
        self.block4 = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Conv2d(128, 64, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True),
            nn.Conv2d(64, 64, kernel_size=3, stride=1, padding=1),
            nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        )
        
        # Final projection to 3 channels at 32x32
        self.output_conv = nn.Conv2d(64, 3, kernel_size=1, stride=1, padding=0)
    
    def forward(self, x):
        """
        Forward pass of the encoder network.
        
        Args:
            x: Input tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
        
        Returns:
            Output tensor of shape [batch, 3, 32, 32]
        """
        # Progressive downsampling through encoder blocks
        x = self.block1(x)  # [batch, 32, 256, 256]
        x = self.block2(x)  # [batch, 64, 128, 128]
        x = self.block3(x)  # [batch, 128, 64, 64]
        x = self.block4(x)  # [batch, 64, 32, 32]
        x = self.output_conv(x)  # [batch, 3, 32, 32]
        
        return x


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Test the network
    model = EncoderNet()
    
    # Create a random input tensor
    batch_size = 4
    input_tensor = torch.randn(batch_size, 3, 512, 512)
    
    # Forward pass
    output_tensor = model(input_tensor)
    
    # Print shapes
    print(f"Input shape: {input_tensor.shape}")
    print(f"Output shape: {output_tensor.shape}")
    
    # Count parameters
    total_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())
    print(f"Total parameters: {total_params:,}")

import torch.nn as nn


class EncoderNet(nn.Module):
    """
    Lightweight encoder network that downsamples input from [batch, 3, 512, 512] to [batch, 3, 32, 32].
    Uses strided convolutions for efficient downsampling (16x reduction in spatial dimensions).
    """
    
    def __init__(self):
        super(EncoderNet, self).__init__()
        
        # Downsample from 512x512 to 32x32 (16x reduction)
        # Using 4 stages with stride 2 each: 512 -> 256 -> 128 -> 64 -> 32
        
        # Stage 1: 512x512 -> 256x256
        self.conv1 = nn.Conv2d(3, 32, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1)
        self.relu1 = nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        
        # Stage 2: 256x256 -> 128x128
        self.conv2 = nn.Conv2d(32, 64, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1)
        self.relu2 = nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        
        # Stage 3: 128x128 -> 64x64
        self.conv3 = nn.Conv2d(64, 64, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1)
        self.relu3 = nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        
        # Stage 4: 64x64 -> 32x32
        self.conv4 = nn.Conv2d(64, 32, kernel_size=3, stride=2, padding=1)
        self.relu4 = nn.ReLU(inplace=True)
        
        # Final projection to 3 channels
        self.conv5 = nn.Conv2d(32, 3, kernel_size=1, stride=1, padding=0)
    
    def forward(self, x):
        """
        Forward pass of the encoder.
        
        Args:
            x: Input tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
        
        Returns:
            Output tensor of shape [batch, 3, 32, 32]
        """
        # Stage 1
        x = self.conv1(x)
        x = self.relu1(x)
        
        # Stage 2
        x = self.conv2(x)
        x = self.relu2(x)
        
        # Stage 3
        x = self.conv3(x)
        x = self.relu3(x)
        
        # Stage 4
        x = self.conv4(x)
        x = self.relu4(x)
        
        # Final projection
        x = self.conv5(x)
        
        return x


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Test the network
    model = EncoderNet()
    
    # Create a sample input
    batch_size = 2
    input_tensor = torch.randn(batch_size, 3, 512, 512)
    
    # Forward pass
    output_tensor = model(input_tensor)
    
    # Print shapes
    print(f"Input shape: {input_tensor.shape}")
    print(f"Output shape: {output_tensor.shape}")
    
    # Count parameters
    total_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())
    print(f"Total parameters: {total_params:,}")

6. 优化生成图像
我们通过梯度下降优化生成图像，使其同时匹配内容和风格。
def run_style_transfer(cnn, content_img, style_img, input_img, num_steps=300,
                       style_weight=1e6, content_weight=1):
    print('Building the style transfer model..')
    model, content_losses, style_losses = get_style_model_and_losses(cnn, content_img, style_img)
    optimizer = optim.LBFGS([input_img.requires_grad_()])

    print('Optimizing..')
    run = [0]
    while run[0] <= num_steps:
        def closure():
            input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)  # 将像素值限制在[0, 1]

            optimizer.zero_grad()
            model(input_img)
            style_score = 0
            content_score = 0

            for sl in style_losses:
                style_score += sl.loss
            for cl in content_losses:
                content_score += cl.loss

            style_score *= style_weight
            content_score *= content_weight

            layer = nn.ReLU(inplace=False)  # 避免inplace操作影响梯度

import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
import torchvision.models as models
import copy


def gram_matrix(input_tensor):
    """
    Compute the Gram matrix for style representation.
    """
    batch, channels, height, width = input_tensor.size()
    features = input_tensor.view(batch * channels, height * width)
    gram = torch.mm(features, features.t())
    return gram.div(batch * channels * height * width)


class ContentLoss(nn.Module):
    """
    Content loss module.
    """
    def __init__(self, target):
        super(ContentLoss, self).__init__()
        self.target = target.detach()
        self.loss = 0

    def forward(self, input_tensor):
        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(input_tensor, self.target)
        return input_tensor


class StyleLoss(nn.Module):
    """
    Style loss module.
    """
    def __init__(self, target_feature):
        super(StyleLoss, self).__init__()
        self.target = gram_matrix(target_feature).detach()
        self.loss = 0

    def forward(self, input_tensor):
        gram = gram_matrix(input_tensor)
        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(gram, self.target)
        return input_tensor


class Normalization(nn.Module):
    """
    Normalization module for VGG preprocessing.
    """
    def __init__(self, mean, std):
        super(Normalization, self).__init__()
        self.mean = mean.view(-1, 1, 1)
        self.std = std.view(-1, 1, 1)

    def forward(self, img):
        return (img - self.mean) / self.std


def get_style_model_and_losses(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
                               content_img, style_img,
                               content_layers=['conv_4'],
                               style_layers=['conv_1', 'conv_2', 'conv_3', 'conv_4', 'conv_5']):
    """
    Build the style transfer model by inserting content and style loss modules.
    """
    normalization = Normalization(normalization_mean, normalization_std).to(content_img.device)
    
    content_losses = []
    style_losses = []

    model = nn.Sequential(normalization)

    i = 0
    for layer in cnn.children():
        if isinstance(layer, nn.Conv2d):
            i += 1
            name = 'conv_{}'.format(i)
        elif isinstance(layer, nn.ReLU):
            name = 'relu_{}'.format(i)
            layer = nn.ReLU(inplace=False)
        elif isinstance(layer, nn.MaxPool2d):
            name = 'pool_{}'.format(i)
        else:
            continue

        model.add_module(name, layer)

        if name in content_layers:
            target = model(content_img).detach()
            content_loss = ContentLoss(target)
            model.add_module('content_loss_{}'.format(i), content_loss)
            content_losses.append(content_loss)

        if name in style_layers:
            target_feature = model(style_img).detach()
            style_loss = StyleLoss(target_feature)
            model.add_module('style_loss_{}'.format(i), style_loss)
            style_losses.append(style_loss)

    for i in range(len(model) - 1, -1, -1):
        if isinstance(model[i], ContentLoss) or isinstance(model[i], StyleLoss):
            break

    model = model[:(i + 1)]

    return model, content_losses, style_losses


def run_style_transfer_single(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
                              content_img, style_img, num_steps=300,
                              style_weight=1e6, content_weight=1):
    """
    Run style transfer for a single content-style pair.
    """
    input_img = content_img.clone()
    
    model, content_losses, style_losses = get_style_model_and_losses(
        cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std, content_img, style_img)
    
    optimizer = optim.LBFGS([input_img.requires_grad_()])

    run = [0]
    while run[0] <= num_steps:
        def closure():
            input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)

            optimizer.zero_grad()
            model(input_img)
            
            style_score = 0
            content_score = 0

            for sl in style_losses:
                style_score += sl.loss
            for cl in content_losses:
                content_score += cl.loss

            style_score *= style_weight
            content_score *= content_weight

            loss = style_score + content_score
            loss.backward()

            run[0] += 1

            return loss

        optimizer.step(closure)

    input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)

    return input_img


def run_style_transfer(content, style):
    """
    Main function for style transfer.
    
    Args:
        content: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
        style: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
    
    Returns:
        Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512] with style-transferred images
    """
    device = content.device
    batch_size = content.size(0)
    
    cnn = models.vgg19(pretrained=True).features.to(device).eval()
    
    normalization_mean = torch.tensor([0.485, 0.456, 0.406]).to(device)
    normalization_std = torch.tensor([0.229, 0.224, 0.225]).to(device)
    
    for param in cnn.parameters():
        param.requires_grad = False
    
    results = []
    
    for i in range(batch_size):
        content_img = content[i:i+1]
        style_img = style[i:i+1]
        
        output_img = run_style_transfer_single(
            cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
            content_img, style_img,
            num_steps=300,
            style_weight=1e6,
            content_weight=1
        )
        
        results.append(output_img)
    
    output = torch.cat(results, dim=0)
    
    return output

import torch.optim as optim
import torchvision.models as models
import copy


def gram_matrix(input_tensor):
    """
    Calculate the Gram matrix for style representation.
    """
    batch, channels, height, width = input_tensor.size()
    features = input_tensor.view(batch * channels, height * width)
    gram = torch.mm(features, features.t())
    return gram.div(batch * channels * height * width)


class ContentLoss(nn.Module):
    """
    Content loss module.
    """
    def __init__(self, target):
        super(ContentLoss, self).__init__()
        self.target = target.detach()
        self.loss = 0

    def forward(self, input_tensor):
        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(input_tensor, self.target)
        return input_tensor


class StyleLoss(nn.Module):
    """
    Style loss module using Gram matrix.
    """
    def __init__(self, target_feature):
        super(StyleLoss, self).__init__()
        self.target = gram_matrix(target_feature).detach()
        self.loss = 0

    def forward(self, input_tensor):
        gram = gram_matrix(input_tensor)
        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(gram, self.target)
        return input_tensor


class Normalization(nn.Module):
    """
    Normalization module for VGG preprocessing.
    """
    def __init__(self, mean, std):
        super(Normalization, self).__init__()
        self.mean = mean.view(-1, 1, 1)
        self.std = std.view(-1, 1, 1)

    def forward(self, img):
        return (img - self.mean) / self.std


def get_style_model_and_losses(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
                               content_img, style_img,
                               content_layers=['conv_4'],
                               style_layers=['conv_1', 'conv_2', 'conv_3', 'conv_4', 'conv_5']):
    """
    Build the style transfer model by inserting content and style loss modules.
    """
    cnn = copy.deepcopy(cnn)
    normalization = Normalization(normalization_mean, normalization_std).to(content_img.device)
    
    content_losses = []
    style_losses = []
    
    model = nn.Sequential(normalization)
    
    i = 0
    for layer in cnn.children():
        if isinstance(layer, nn.Conv2d):
            i += 1
            name = 'conv_{}'.format(i)
        elif isinstance(layer, nn.ReLU):
            name = 'relu_{}'.format(i)
            layer = nn.ReLU(inplace=False)
        elif isinstance(layer, nn.MaxPool2d):
            name = 'pool_{}'.format(i)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError('Unrecognized layer: {}'.format(layer.__class__.__name__))
        
        model.add_module(name, layer)
        
        if name in content_layers:
            target = model(content_img).detach()
            content_loss = ContentLoss(target)
            model.add_module('content_loss_{}'.format(i), content_loss)
            content_losses.append(content_loss)
        
        if name in style_layers:
            target_feature = model(style_img).detach()
            style_loss = StyleLoss(target_feature)
            model.add_module('style_loss_{}'.format(i), style_loss)
            style_losses.append(style_loss)
    
    for i in range(len(model) - 1, -1, -1):
        if isinstance(model[i], ContentLoss) or isinstance(model[i], StyleLoss):
            break
    
    model = model[:(i + 1)]
    
    return model, content_losses, style_losses


def run_style_transfer_single(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
                              content_img, style_img, input_img, num_steps=300,
                              style_weight=1e6, content_weight=1):
    """
    Run style transfer optimization for a single image pair.
    """
    model, content_losses, style_losses = get_style_model_and_losses(
        cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std, content_img, style_img)
    
    optimizer = optim.LBFGS([input_img.requires_grad_()])
    
    run = [0]
    while run[0] <= num_steps:
        def closure():
            input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)
            
            optimizer.zero_grad()
            model(input_img)
            
            style_score = 0
            content_score = 0
            
            for sl in style_losses:
                style_score += sl.loss
            for cl in content_losses:
                content_score += cl.loss
            
            style_score *= style_weight
            content_score *= content_weight
            
            loss = style_score + content_score
            loss.backward()
            
            run[0] += 1
            
            return style_score + content_score
        
        optimizer.step(closure)
    
    input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)
    
    return input_img


def run_style_transfer(content, style):
    """
    Main function for style transfer.
    
    Args:
        content: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
        style: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
    
    Returns:
        Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512] with style-transferred images
    """
    device = content.device
    batch_size = content.size(0)
    
    cnn = models.vgg19(pretrained=True).features.to(device).eval()
    
    for param in cnn.parameters():
        param.requires_grad = False
    
    normalization_mean = torch.tensor([0.485, 0.456, 0.406]).to(device)
    normalization_std = torch.tensor([0.229, 0.224, 0.225]).to(device)
    
    results = []
    
    for i in range(batch_size):
        content_img = content[i:i+1]
        style_img = style[i:i+1]
        
        input_img = content_img.clone()
        
        output_img = run_style_transfer_single(
            cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
            content_img, style_img, input_img,
            num_steps=300,
            style_weight=1e6,
            content_weight=1
        )
        
        results.append(output_img)
    
    return torch.cat(results, dim=0)

     1	import torch
     2	import torch.nn as nn
     3	import torch.optim as optim
     4	import torchvision.models as models
     5	import copy

     7	
     8	def gram_matrix(input_tensor):
     9	    """
    10	    Compute the Gram matrix for style representation.
    11	    """
    12	    batch, channels, height, width = input_tensor.size()
    13	    features = input_tensor.view(batch * channels, height * width)
    14	    gram = torch.mm(features, features.t())
    15	    return gram.div(batch * channels * height * width)
    16	
    17	
    18	class ContentLoss(nn.Module):
    19	    """
    20	    Content loss module.
    21	    """
    22	    def __init__(self, target):
    23	        super(ContentLoss, self).__init__()
    24	        self.target = target.detach()
    25	        self.loss = 0
    26	
    27	    def forward(self, input_tensor):
    28	        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(input_tensor, self.target)
    29	        return input_tensor
    30	
    31	
    32	class StyleLoss(nn.Module):
    33	    """
    34	    Style loss module.
    35	    """
    36	    def __init__(self, target_feature):
    37	        super(StyleLoss, self).__init__()
    38	        self.target = gram_matrix(target_feature).detach()
    39	        self.loss = 0
    40	
    41	    def forward(self, input_tensor):
    42	        gram = gram_matrix(input_tensor)
    43	        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(gram, self.target)
    44	        return input_tensor
    45	
    46	
    47	class Normalization(nn.Module):
    48	    """
    49	    Normalization module for VGG preprocessing.
    50	    """
    51	    def __init__(self, mean, std):
    52	        super(Normalization, self).__init__()
    53	        self.mean = mean.view(-1, 1, 1)
    54	        self.std = std.view(-1, 1, 1)
    55	
    56	    def forward(self, img):
    57	        return (img - self.mean) / self.std
    58	
    59	
    60	def get_style_model_and_losses(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
    61	                               content_img, style_img,
    62	                               content_layers=['conv_4'],
    63	                               style_layers=['conv_1', 'conv_2', 'conv_3', 'conv_4', 'conv_5']):
    64	    """
    65	    Build the style transfer model by inserting content and style loss modules.
    66	    """
    67	    cnn = copy.deepcopy(cnn)
    68	    normalization = Normalization(normalization_mean, normalization_std).to(content_img.device)
    69	    
    70	    content_losses = []
    71	    style_losses = []
    72	
    73	    model = nn.Sequential(normalization)
    74	
    75	    i = 0
    76	    for layer in cnn.children():
    77	        if isinstance(layer, nn.Conv2d):
    78	            i += 1
    79	            name = 'conv_{}'.format(i)
    80	        elif isinstance(layer, nn.ReLU):
    81	            name = 'relu_{}'.format(i)
    82	            layer = nn.ReLU(inplace=False)
    83	        elif isinstance(layer, nn.MaxPool2d):
    84	            name = 'pool_{}'.format(i)
    85	        else:
    86	            continue
    87	
    88	        model.add_module(name, layer)
    89	
    90	        if name in content_layers:
    91	            target = model(content_img).detach()
    92	            content_loss = ContentLoss(target)
    93	            model.add_module('content_loss_{}'.format(i), content_loss)
    94	            content_losses.append(content_loss)
    95	
    96	        if name in style_layers:
    97	            target_feature = model(style_img).detach()
    98	            style_loss = StyleLoss(target_feature)
    99	            model.add_module('style_loss_{}'.format(i), style_loss)
   100	            style_losses.append(style_loss)
   101	
   102	    for i in range(len(model) - 1, -1, -1):
   103	        if isinstance(model[i], ContentLoss) or isinstance(model[i], StyleLoss):
   104	            break
   105	
   106	    model = model[:(i + 1)]
   107	
   108	    return model, content_losses, style_losses
   109	
   110	
   111	def run_style_transfer_single(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
   112	                              content_img, style_img, num_steps=300,
   113	                              style_weight=1e6, content_weight=1):
   114	    """
   115	    Run style transfer for a single content-style pair.
   116	    """
   117	    input_img = content_img.clone()
   118	    
   119	    model, content_losses, style_losses = get_style_model_and_losses(
   120	        cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std, content_img, style_img)
   121	    
   122	    optimizer = optim.LBFGS([input_img.requires_grad_()])
   123	
   124	    run = [0]
   125	    while run[0] <= num_steps:
   126	        def closure():
   127	            input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)
   128	
   129	            optimizer.zero_grad()
   130	            model(input_img)
   131	            
   132	            style_score = 0
   133	            content_score = 0
   134	
   135	            for sl in style_losses:
   136	                style_score += sl.loss
   137	            for cl in content_losses:
   138	                content_score += cl.loss
   139	
   140	            style_score *= style_weight
   141	            content_score *= content_weight
   142	
   143	            loss = style_score + content_score
   144	            loss.backward()
   145	
   146	            run[0] += 1
   147	
   148	            return loss
   149	
   150	        optimizer.step(closure)
   151	
   152	    input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)
   153	
   154	    return input_img
   155	
   156	
   157	def run_style_transfer(content, style):
   158	    """
   159	    Main function for style transfer.
   160	    
   161	    Args:
   162	        content: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
   163	        style: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
   164	    
   165	    Returns:
   166	        Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512] with style-transferred images
   167	    """
   168	    device = content.device
   169	    batch_size = content.size(0)
   170	    
   171	    cnn = models.vgg19(pretrained=True).features.to(device).eval()
   172	    
   173	    normalization_mean = torch.tensor([0.485, 0.456, 0.406]).to(device)
   174	    normalization_std = torch.tensor([0.229, 0.224, 0.225]).to(device)
   175	    
   176	    for param in cnn.parameters():
   177	        param.requires_grad = False
   178	    
   179	    results = []
   180	    
   181	    for i in range(batch_size):
   182	        content_img = content[i:i+1]
   183	        style_img = style[i:i+1]
   184	        
   185	        output_img = run_style_transfer_single(
   186	            cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
   187	            content_img, style_img,
   188	            num_steps=300,
   189	            style_weight=1e6,
   190	            content_weight=1
   191	        )
   192	        
   193	        results.append(output_img)
   194	    
   195	    output = torch.cat(results, dim=0)
   196	    
   197	    return output

Perfect! I have successfully created the style transfer implementation in `/styleCode.py`. Here's a summary of what was implemented:

## Implementation Summary

The code implements neural style transfer using PyTorch with the following key components:

**Main Function**: `run_style_transfer(content, style)`
- Accepts two tensors of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
- Processes each content-style pair individually
- Returns concatenated results of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]

**Key Features**:
1. **VGG19 Network**: Uses pretrained VGG19 for feature extraction
2. **Gram Matrix**: Computes style representation via Gram matrices
3. **Loss Functions**:
   - Content loss: MSE between content features (conv_4 layer)
   - Style loss: MSE between Gram matrices (conv_1 through conv_5 layers)
4. **Optimization**: LBFGS optimizer for 300 iterations
5. **Hyperparameters**: 
   - style_weight = 1e6
   - content_weight = 1
   - num_steps = 300

**Architecture**:
- No batch normalization layers (as requested)
- No special symbols or emojis in the code
- Follows standard PyTorch style transfer patterns
- Uses ImageNet normalization (mean=[0.485, 0.456, 0.406], std=[0.229, 0.224, 0.225])

The file is saved directly at `/styleCode.py` without any parent folders.

================================ Human Message =================================

Write a function that calculates the content loss between input_img and content_img, and the style_loss between input_img and style_img.
Inputs:
- input_img: The style_transferred images with dimensions of [batch, 3, 512, 512].
- content_img: The content images with dimensions of [batch, 3, 512, 512].
- style_img: The style images with dimensions of [batch, 3, 512, 512].
Output:
- content_score: Content loss value with dimensions of [batch].
- style_score: Style loss value with dimensions of [batch].

The reference code:
```Python
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
import torchvision.models as models
import copy


def gram_matrix(input_tensor):
    """
    Compute the Gram matrix for style representation.
    """
    batch, channels, height, width = input_tensor.size()
    features = input_tensor.view(batch * channels, height * width)
    gram = torch.mm(features, features.t())
    return gram.div(batch * channels * height * width)


class ContentLoss(nn.Module):
    """
    Content loss module.
    """
    def __init__(self, target):
        super(ContentLoss, self).__init__()
        self.target = target.detach()
        self.loss = 0

    def forward(self, input_tensor):
        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(input_tensor, self.target)
        return input_tensor


class StyleLoss(nn.Module):
    """
    Style loss module.
    """
    def __init__(self, target_feature):
        super(StyleLoss, self).__init__()
        self.target = gram_matrix(target_feature).detach()
        self.loss = 0

    def forward(self, input_tensor):
        gram = gram_matrix(input_tensor)
        self.loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(gram, self.target)
        return input_tensor


class Normalization(nn.Module):
    """
    Normalization module for VGG preprocessing.
    """
    def __init__(self, mean, std):
        super(Normalization, self).__init__()
        self.mean = mean.view(-1, 1, 1)
        self.std = std.view(-1, 1, 1)

    def forward(self, img):
        return (img - self.mean) / self.std


def get_style_model_and_losses(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
                               content_img, style_img,
                               content_layers=['conv_4'],
                               style_layers=['conv_1', 'conv_2', 'conv_3', 'conv_4', 'conv_5']):
    """
    Build the style transfer model by inserting content and style loss modules.
    """
    cnn = copy.deepcopy(cnn)
    normalization = Normalization(normalization_mean, normalization_std).to(content_img.device)
    
    content_losses = []
    style_losses = []

    model = nn.Sequential(normalization)

    i = 0
    for layer in cnn.children():
        if isinstance(layer, nn.Conv2d):
            i += 1
            name = 'conv_{}'.format(i)
        elif isinstance(layer, nn.ReLU):
            name = 'relu_{}'.format(i)
            layer = nn.ReLU(inplace=False)
        elif isinstance(layer, nn.MaxPool2d):
            name = 'pool_{}'.format(i)
        else:
            continue

        model.add_module(name, layer)

        if name in content_layers:
            target = model(content_img).detach()
            content_loss = ContentLoss(target)
            model.add_module('content_loss_{}'.format(i), content_loss)
            content_losses.append(content_loss)

        if name in style_layers:
            target_feature = model(style_img).detach()
            style_loss = StyleLoss(target_feature)
            model.add_module('style_loss_{}'.format(i), style_loss)
            style_losses.append(style_loss)

    for i in range(len(model) - 1, -1, -1):
        if isinstance(model[i], ContentLoss) or isinstance(model[i], StyleLoss):
            break

    model = model[:(i + 1)]

    return model, content_losses, style_losses


def run_style_transfer_single(cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
                              content_img, style_img, num_steps=300,
                              style_weight=1e6, content_weight=1):
    """
    Run style transfer for a single content-style pair.
    """
    input_img = content_img.clone()
    
    model, content_losses, style_losses = get_style_model_and_losses(
        cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std, content_img, style_img)
    
    optimizer = optim.LBFGS([input_img.requires_grad_()])

    run = [0]
    while run[0] <= num_steps:
        def closure():
            input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)

            optimizer.zero_grad()
            model(input_img)
            
            style_score = 0
            content_score = 0

            for sl in style_losses:
                style_score += sl.loss
            for cl in content_losses:
                content_score += cl.loss

            style_score *= style_weight
            content_score *= content_weight

            loss = style_score + content_score
            loss.backward()

            run[0] += 1

            return loss

        optimizer.step(closure)

    input_img.data.clamp_(0, 1)

    return input_img


def run_style_transfer(content, style):
    """
    Main function for style transfer.
    
    Args:
        content: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
        style: Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512]
    
    Returns:
        Tensor of shape [batch, 3, 512, 512] with style-transferred images
    """
    device = content.device
    batch_size = content.size(0)
    
    cnn = models.vgg19(pretrained=True).features.to(device).eval()
    
    normalization_mean = torch.tensor([0.485, 0.456, 0.406]).to(device)
    normalization_std = torch.tensor([0.229, 0.224, 0.225]).to(device)
    
    for param in cnn.parameters():
        param.requires_grad = False
    
    results = []
    
    for i in range(batch_size):
        content_img = content[i:i+1]
        style_img = style[i:i+1]
        
        output_img = run_style_transfer_single(
            cnn, normalization_mean, normalization_std,
            content_img, style_img,
            num_steps=300,
            style_weight=1e6,
            content_weight=1
        )
        
        results.append(output_img)
    
    output = torch.cat(results, dim=0)
    
    return output


```

1. Preserve the content of the reference code as much as possible.
2. Only return the content loss value and the style loss value. Do not optimize any parameters.
3. Define the file name of the code as StyleLoss.py.
4. Define the main function as **func_loss**.
